# Ambiguity Register (200+)

> SYNTHETIC-DATA REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION. Not CDF production, not regulatory certification, and not final HR or legal conformance. Written 2026-10-13 for the owner appendix "CDF 200-Ambiguities Resolution Engine v1.0". Linear [CDF-204](https://linear.app/cdfcasemanagement/issue/CDF-204); the follow-on decisions DEC-AMB-013…015 and row AMB-251 were added the same day ([CDF-210](https://linear.app/cdfcasemanagement/issue/CDF-210)); the built state of migration 2070 and the suites the 2060 closure tests landed in were added on 2026-10-15 ([CDF-223](https://linear.app/cdfcasemanagement/issue/CDF-223)); the built state of the 93 rows placed in 2030/2040, 2050 and 2060, and of 6 rows that 2045 or 2065 touched, was re-checked on 2026-10-16 against main 0f59062, which the Hosted DEV (Supabase) run [38804164091](https://github.com/mh1m1m/CDF_Case_Management/actions/runs/38804164091) tested green ([CDF-224](https://linear.app/cdfcasemanagement/issue/CDF-224)); the placements of the eight gap rows tracked as CDF-239…246, and Fady's decisions of 2026-10-16 on AMB-113 and AMB-146, were added on 2026-10-16 ([CDF-248](https://linear.app/cdfcasemanagement/issue/CDF-248)). Generated from one data set with its companion files, so counts agree across them.

Companion files: [OWNER_DECISIONS_FROM_AMBIGUITIES.md](OWNER_DECISIONS_FROM_AMBIGUITIES.md) (R5), [SOURCE_REQUIRED_REGISTER.md](SOURCE_REQUIRED_REGISTER.md) (R4), [CONFIGURATION_REQUIREMENTS.md](CONFIGURATION_REQUIREMENTS.md) (R3), [AMBIGUITY_TEST_MATRIX.md](AMBIGUITY_TEST_MATRIX.md) (CRITICAL/HIGH → tests), [CONTRADICTION_REGISTER.md](CONTRADICTION_REGISTER.md).

### Distribution against the appendix targets (§8)

| Class                   | Target | Actual | Met? |
| ----------------------- | ------ | ------ | ---- |
| R1 already decided      | ≥ 25%  | 31.9%  | Yes  |
| R2 safe derivation      | ≥ 20%  | 31.9%  | Yes  |
| R3 configurable default | ≥ 20%  | 17.5%  | No   |
| R4 SOURCE_REQUIRED      | ≥ 15%  | 13.9%  | No   |
| R5 owner decision       | ≤ 20%  | 4.8%   | Yes  |

R3 and R4 fall short of their expected shares because the owner's Finalization Set (DEC-F01–F41, 2026-10-13) already settled many items that would otherwise be configurable defaults or source requests. Those items are counted as R1, which is what they now are; reclassifying them to meet a quota would misstate the evidence. Where an R1 decision itself leaves a value SOURCE_REQUIRED (for example DEC-F18, DEC-F22, DEC-F31), the value is listed in the second table of SOURCE_REQUIRED_REGISTER.md, and every owner-named setting is listed in CONFIGURATION_REQUIREMENTS.md.

The domain minimums add up to 340; §7 asks for the best 200–250 material items rather than filler, so every domain is covered and below-minimum domains are ones where the Q&A, the ADRs or the Finalization Set already close most questions. 15 low-value candidates were merged or dropped during de-duplication (§29), for example "who approves minutes" (Q97) and "is remote attendance allowed" (ADR-020), because an existing row already answers them. The register was delivered with 250 rows; AMB-251, found later the same day, brings it to 251 (see "How to read").

## How to read this register

- **Resolution order (§2):** confirmed owner decision → explicit requirement → ADR → governance document → security invariant → safe default → configurable rule → SOURCE_REQUIRED → owner decision. Only R5 goes to the owner.
- **R5** rows were all decided by Fady on 2026-10-13 (DEC-AMB-001…012), and he then decided three follow-on questions on rows that already had a decision: DEC-AMB-013 and DEC-AMB-014 on AMB-051, and DEC-AMB-015 on AMB-051 and AMB-218. Each row shows his decision text unedited, its placement, and what holds until the placement lands: a fail-safe that is built today stays until the replacement rule and its negative tests are in place. AMB-051 also carries the two neighbouring consequences he kept as coordinator defaults, marked as owner-confirmed defaults and not as decisions.
- **Added after delivery:** AMB-251 (Grievance, R4) was recorded by the rebase thread while it built migration 2040 and is appended with an explicit id, so no row or test ID changes; it is listed in its domain, and its test is T-AMB-151.
- **R1** cites the Q&A number (Q1–Q100), the owner decision (DEC-01…DEC-68 in the UX audit register O17 §17.7 and ADR-016; DEC-F01…DEC-F41 from the Finalization Set of 2026-10-13; DEC-61 and DEC-62 owner cards in ADR-018 and ADR-022) or a settled coordinator ruling. **R1 items are never asked again.**
- **"already open as DEC-nn; not re-asked"** marks a question that the UX audit register already put to the owner and that is still open. This register applies a fail-safe default and does not raise it again.
- **Evidence** names ADRs (`architecture/adr/`), migrations by their last four digits (for example 1890 = `20261007001890_committee_meetings_votes_minutes.sql`), Linear issues, legacy form and CRM status codes, and negative tests N1–N20 from AUTHORIZATION_IMPACT.md.
- **Implementation impact** names the owner: the rebase thread's PR-A (2030/2040, applying DEC-F01–F41) and PR-B (2050 evidence half, 2060 closure, 2070 P11-G3, 2080 P11-G7), the UX thread (2020 access narrowing, Phase 12 screens), or "unallocated" when the coordinator must assign a number. The owner decisions are placed in 2030, 2040, 2050, 2060, 2120 (ADR-048) and 2130 (ADR-049). This register writes no migration.
- **Audit (§24):** every resolution that changes permission, workflow, decision, deadline, evidence or committee participation names its audit event in the implementation column or relies on the command's existing same-transaction audit event (ADR-003, ADR-005). Audit metadata carries codes and ids, never reasons or content (coordinator ruling).
- **Tests (§25):** every CRITICAL and HIGH row has a test ID (T-AMB-nnn) in AMBIGUITY_TEST_MATRIX.md; security rows carry a negative test. EXISTS means a test in the named file already asserts it on main 7130104; EXTEND means an existing test covers part of it and the missing case is named; NEW means it must be written. Every EXISTS and EXTEND claim was checked against its suite by reading it on 2026-10-13; the suites were not run for this document. For the rows re-checked with CDF-224 the status describes main 0f59062 instead, the commit the Hosted DEV (Supabase) workflow ran the full database suite on, green, in run [38804164091](https://github.com/mh1m1m/CDF_Case_Management/actions/runs/38804164091) (2026-10-16); their note starts "Re-checked on main 0f59062" or says what was found there.
- **Since delivery:** 99 rows were re-checked against main 0f59062 on 2026-10-16 (CDF-224): the 93 rows placed in 2030/2040, 2050 and 2060, and 6 rows that 2045 or 2065 touched. Each says in its implementation column whether main built it (72 built, 19 partly built, 5 not built, 3 built before the register was written), with the migration, ADR, pull request and merge date, then what is not built, and its test column names the suite and title found there. AMB-021 says what 2070 built (CDF-223). Every other row still describes main 7130104 and the plan of 2026-10-13. Where the register named a setting that main built under another name or as a table, the row says so; the register's own setting column is unchanged.
- **Gap placements (CDF-248):** the eight rows whose gaps CDF-224 left as "placement pending" (AMB-030, AMB-109, AMB-113, AMB-129, AMB-146, AMB-152, AMB-175 and AMB-202, tracked as CDF-239…246) now say in their implementation column where each gap is built, as the coordinator ruled on 2026-10-16, or what it is blocked on. Fady decided two gaps left after delivery on 2026-10-16 (CDF-248): AMB-113 (CDF-241, option B) and AMB-146 (CDF-246, option A). They settle how a gap is built, not how the row resolves, so they are not R5 decisions: no class, resolution, risk or test ID changes, and the coordinator placed the other six gap rows itself. His words are quoted under "Owner decisions on gap rows" at the end and in OWNER_DECISIONS_FROM_AMBIGUITIES.md.

| AMB-030 | Subjects, allegations, findings | Can a finding be changed after the committee voted on it?                                                   | Editing an adopted finding changes a decided matter.                                         | ADR-020 (vote items, rounds), ADR-021 (rounds); finding.version_no (1870); ADR-040 §3 item 8                                                                      | R2               | An adopted finding version is frozen. Change needs a new version adopted in a new vote round before the authority decides; after the decision only through grievance or reinvestigation.                                                                                                                                                                  | No                                                     | HIGH   | No                               | Partly built before the register was written, in migrations 1870 and 1890 (ADR-040 §3 item 8). Findings are append-only versions in `case_mgmt.finding` (1870), and a FINDING vote item binds one version through `committee.vote_item.finding_id`, which cannot change once the item is closed (1890). A new version recorded with `api.record_finding` is a separate row awaiting its own vote, and the version bound to the closed vote item stays unchanged. Not built: Nothing confines a change after the authority decision to grievance or reinvestigation. api.record_finding (1870, not replaced) records a new finding version on any proceeding that is not CONCLUDED or CANCELLED, with no check on the package or decision status. api.open_vote_item (2060) opens a FINDING vote on that version in an open meeting. api.create_recommendation_package (1900) opens a new package while the committee proceeding is OPEN. Only the decided package and the version bound to its closed vote item are frozen. Tracked as CDF-239, placed in migration 2120 (ADR-048) as a refusal that fails closed once the package is decided, after 2099; in a migration of its own (2180) if 2120 is too large. | AMB-031 | Subjects, allegations, findings | What happens when the subject leaves employment during the case?                                            | Continuing may be pointless or unlawful; stopping may lose governance findings.              | No Q&A answer; DEC-F11 (sanction bases SOURCE_REQUIRED); Q72 (corrective actions remain possible); ADR-040 §5 item 8; ADR-040 §5 item 10 (migration 2040, PR #67) | R5               | Decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option A: "If a subject leaves employment during the case, continue the investigation through findings and recommendations. Applicable sanctions may be recorded but must not be shown as executable where the employment relationship no longer permits execution. Governance/corrective actions remain actionable." | No                                                     | HIGH   | Decided 2026-10-13 (DEC-AMB-009) | Decided; built in migration 2040 (ADR-040 §5 item 8; ADR-040 §5 item 10), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). Migration 2040 adds `api.flag_subject_left_employment` (a case editor, with a source reference), which writes the append-only `case_mgmt.subject_employment_event` (LEFT_EMPLOYMENT). Findings, recommendations, adoption and the authority decision carry on unchanged, and governance and corrective actions stay actionable. `decision.execution_block` is the one source for the `decision.sanction_execution` guard trigger and for `api.sanction_executability`. It returns `SUBJECT_LEFT_EMPLOYMENT`, so the sanction is neither executable nor shown as executable, unless `api.record_employment_execution_statement` has recorded, with a source document, that the employment relationship still permits that sanction type for that subject. There is no authority override. Migration 2050 adds `api.request_sanction_extract(committee proceeding, subject, reason)` for the secretary of an OPEN proceeding (cleared for the case, with no meeting conflict; otherwise FORBIDDEN or `PROCEEDING_NOT_OPEN`). It fills the append-only `decision.sanction_extract` and `decision.sanction_extract_row` (no delete; a row changes only once, when an AWAITING_AUTHORITY row is approved (INCLUDED) or withheld (WITHHELD)) from `decision.final_sanctions_of_person`: sanctions that were executed and are in effect, whose decision is not blocked or superseded, whose grievance window has ended, and that no grievance holds open or has set aside. `api.sanction_extract_view` gives the proceeding's secretary and seated members without a meeting conflict only the position, sanction code, whether it is a catalogue sanction (`catalogue_sanction`) and two dates within their clearance (with the extract's own ids, requester and time), never a case, allegation, finding, evidence or history; each view is audited `SANCTION_EXTRACT_VIEWED`, and anyone else gets NOT_FOUND. A row from a restricted case stays `AWAITING_AUTHORITY`, invisible, until that case's Restricted Case Authority approves it with `api.decide_sanction_extract_row` (never the requester: `REQUESTER_CANNOT_CONSENT`). No `authz.*` predicate is widened, so a committee member still cannot discover the subject's other cases. Owner: Rebase PR-B, 2050 (P11-G4 evidence half). | T-AMB-015: A committee member cannot discover the subject's other cases. | AMB-037 | Committee registry & membership | Which rules apply when a committee rule set changes mid-case?                    | A rule change could rewrite the quorum of a past vote.                   | Q27, Q30, Q53; ADR-018 §2 (rule_snapshot immutable); appendix §13                                                                       | R1               | The rule snapshot taken at referral governs the proceeding. Later rule versions never reach it.                                                                                                                                                         | No                                                                 | HIGH   | No                     | None (built).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | T-AMB-017: A rule-set change after referral does not change the proceeding's snapshot. `tests/security/committee-registry.spec.ts` (EXISTS)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| AMB-038 | Committee registry & membership | Can a member appointed after the proceeding started take part?                   | Silent additions change the composition that decides.                    | Q30; ADR-018 §2 (snapshot amendment row, PROCEEDING_SNAPSHOT_AMENDED); ADR-040 §2 item 5 (migration 2030, PR #67)                       | R2               | Only through an audited snapshot amendment with a reason and the case gate; never automatically.                                                                                                                                                        | No                                                                 | HIGH   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §2 item 5), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). A member appointed after the referral joins a proceeding only through `api.amend_proceeding_snapshot`: the committee's secretary, with a reason (10 to 2000 characters), adds them to `committee.proceeding_member`, records `committee.proceeding_snapshot_amendment` and the audit event `PROCEEDING_SNAPSHOT_AMENDED`, and is refused for a member not cleared for the case (`NOT_CLEARED_FOR_CASE`), one already in the snapshot (`ALREADY_IN_SNAPSHOT`) and any amendment on a restricted case (`AUTHORITY_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`). Until then `authz.can_view_committee_proceeding` gives them no view of the proceeding and scheduling copies only snapshot members into meetings. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                  | T-AMB-018: A newly appointed member gets no proceeding access without an amendment (negative). `tests/security/owner-decisions-meetings.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "a member appointed after the referral joins later meetings only by the secretary's recorded amendment" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| AMB-039 | Committee registry & membership | What happens to a member whose seat ends during an open proceeding?              | An ex-member could keep voting.                                          | Q27, Q30; ADR-019 §5; N3; ADR-040 §2 item 5 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                                                    | R2               | The snapshot row stays as history. From the end date the person is not eligible for new meetings, votes or packets of the proceeding; past votes stand.                                                                                                 | No                                                                 | HIGH   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §2 item 5), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `committee.member_seat_current` ties participation to the current seat: `authz.can_view_committee_proceeding` and `authz.can_view_meeting_deliberation` (and with them the proceeding's packages and packets) close from the seat's end, `committee.insert_meeting` copies only still-seated members into new meetings, and `api.record_attendance` refuses `SEAT_ENDED` for PRESENT; `api.cast_vote` and `api.revise_vote` refuse `NOT_FOUND` (the vote item is no longer visible, as `committee.lock_vote_item` checks `authz.can_view_meeting_deliberation`), with a `SEAT_ENDED` check behind it. The `committee.proceeding_member` row stays as history, votes on items closed before the end stand (a vote cast on an item still open when the seat ends is left out of the tally at close), and quorum counts only current seats (`committee.counts_as_voter`). Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application). | T-AMB-019: A member whose seat ended cannot be invited, vote or open packets (negative). `tests/security/owner-decisions-meetings.spec.ts` (EXTEND, negative; missing: No test opens a decision packet as the member whose seat ended, and the test does not try `api.invite_to_meeting`, which has no seat check: a member whose seat ended can still be invited as a non-voting invitee, so 'cannot be invited' holds only for the copy of members at scheduling (`committee.insert_meeting`); it asserts no vote, no deliberation, no proceeding view and no copy into the next meeting. Found on main 0f59062: "a member whose seat ends loses the proceeding's meetings and votes at once" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).) |
| AMB-040 | Committee registry & membership | How is an acting chair activated for a meeting?                                  | DEC-F03 requires formal activation, which nothing records yet.           | DEC-F03; Q25; ADR-019 §4 (delegation, not built — see CON-007); ADR-040 §1; ADR-040 §2 item 2 (migration 2030, PR #67)                  | R3               | The secretary records, at meeting opening, that the deputy chair acts as chair because the chair is absent, conflicted or ineligible, with the reason. The acting chair has the casting vote only if the rule set grants it.                            | Yes — `ACTING_CHAIR_CASTING_VOTE = false (per committee rule set)` | HIGH   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §1; ADR-040 §2 item 2), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `api.activate_acting_chair` lets the secretary running the meeting record in `committee.meeting_acting_role` that the deputy chair acts as chair, with a reason (10 to 2000 characters) and a basis derived from the records (ABSENT, CONFLICTED, INELIGIBLE or VACANT; `CHAIR_AVAILABLE` otherwise, `NO_ELIGIBLE_DEPUTY` without an eligible deputy), and writes `ACTING_CHAIR_ACTIVATED` with ids and the basis only. `committee.casting_voter` gives the activated acting chair the casting vote only when the rule snapshot's `acting_chair_casting_vote` is true; it ships false (SOURCE_REQUIRED). Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | T-AMB-020: Casting vote by an unactivated deputy, or by an acting chair when the rule disallows it, is refused (negative). `tests/security/owner-decisions-meetings.spec.ts` (EXTEND, negative; missing: No test has a deputy chair who was never activated attempt the casting vote; the activated acting chair is refused with the rule off here and casts with it on in the next test. Found on main 0f59062: "the secretary activates the deputy for an absent chair; the deputy casts only when the rule grants it" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                        |
| AMB-041 | Committee registry & membership | When does an alternate member vote, and how is double counting prevented?        | An alternate and the member they replace could both count toward quorum. | Q26; ADR-019 §1 (ALTERNATE_MEMBER); Q51; ADR-040 §2 item 3 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                                     | R3               | An alternate votes only when activated for a meeting in place of a named absent or conflicted member; quorum counts seats, so the replaced member and the alternate never both count.                                                                   | Yes — `ALTERNATE_ACTIVATION = SECRETARY_WITH_REASON`               | HIGH   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §2 item 3), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `api.activate_alternate_member` lets the secretary running an OPEN meeting, with no vote in progress and a reason (10 to 2000 characters), activate an ALTERNATE_MEMBER seat holder in place of a named voting MEMBER who is conflicted, ineligible or recorded absent or excused (`MEMBER_AVAILABLE` otherwise), audited as `ALTERNATE_MEMBER_ACTIVATED`. Quorum and the tally count seats (`committee.counts_as_voter`): the replaced member is refused `REPLACED_BY_ALTERNATE` on `api.record_attendance` (PRESENT) and on `api.cast_vote`, so the two never both count. There is no ALTERNATE_ACTIVATION setting: the secretary-with-reason rule is fixed in the command. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                          | T-AMB-021: An activated alternate and the replaced member cannot both be PRESENT and voting (negative). `tests/security/owner-decisions-meetings.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "an alternate votes in place of an absent member; the replaced member is not counted or recorded present" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

A new secretary takes over by appointment; open work items follow the seat.                                                   | No                                                                 | MEDIUM | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §2 item 2), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `authz.can_manage_meeting` now wraps `committee.manages_meeting`: the committee's current SECRETARY seat holder runs a meeting, and an ALTERNATE_SECRETARY only while recorded as ACTING_SECRETARY in `committee.meeting_acting_role` by `api.activate_acting_secretary` (the secretary handing over, or the meeting's chair or acting chair), with a reason. Without an eligible alternate the command refuses `NO_ELIGIBLE_ALTERNATE` and nobody else can open or run the meeting; because the checks read the current seat, a newly appointed secretary takes over the meeting's work. | AMB-047 | Committee registry & membership | Do pending tasks follow the person or the seat when a role holder changes?       | Work stranded with a former secretary stops the proceeding.              | ADR-030 (My Committees from effective seats); ADR-016 D0 (My Work); ADR-051, "Context"; ADR-051 §1; ADR-051 §2 (migration 2045, PR #73) | R2               | Work follows the seat: My Work items derive from the current effective seat.                                                                                                                                                                            | No                                                                 | MEDIUM | No                     | Built in migration 2045 (ADR-051, "Context"; ADR-051 §1; ADR-051 §2), on main since PR #73 (merged 2026-10-14). `api.my_work_items()` derives committee work from the caller's current effective seat. Meeting and declaration rows require `committee.member_seat_current` for a member (an invitee such as a witness needs no seat), and vote rows always require it, and a row is filed under the Committee area only while `authz.user_committee_seat` names a current seat. `api.my_areas()` shows My Committees (`COMMITTEE`) only for a current seat of an ACTIVE user; a past seat or a place in a proceeding snapshot gives no area and no work. Referral, escalation and grievance rows reuse the seat-based queues of 2040 (`api.referral_queue()`, `api.grievance_queue()`). Owner: UX thread, S4 work items and notifications.                                                                                                                                                                                       | No test was planned; one landed: `tests/security/my-work-phase12.spec.ts` (EXISTS; Re-checked on main 0f59062: "a scheduled meeting: attendance and the declaration each participant owes, for seated participants only" (PR #73, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

`authz.meeting_concern_decider_seat`) names the decider of a raised conflict in order: the chair, else the deputy chair, else the committee's independent authority in force in `committee.conflict_authority`. The chair or deputy must be cleared for the case and not conflicted, absent or unseated, the authority must be cleared for the case, hold no seat and not be in the meeting, and the raiser and the subject never decide. The authority is appointed by `api.appoint_conflict_authority` (COMMITTEE_ADMIN; `SELF_APPOINTMENT`, `AUTHORITY_NOT_INDEPENDENT`, `AUTHORITY_OVERLAP`) with source status SOURCE_REQUIRED or APPROVED, and none ships appointed, so with no eligible decider the concern stays open and no vote item opens (`CONFLICT_CONCERN_OPEN`). A secretary with a declared conflict in the meeting no longer runs it (`committee.manages_meeting` behind `authz.can_manage_meeting`) or manages the proceeding's packages (`authz.can_manage_recommendation_package` excludes anyone with a meeting conflict in the proceeding). The alternate secretary then runs the meeting only once activated with `api.activate_acting_secretary` (basis CONFLICTED), by the conflicted secretary themself (handover) or by the chair or acting chair; with no eligible alternate it refuses `NO_ELIGIBLE_ALTERNATE` and the meeting cannot proceed. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | T-AMB-027: A conflicted secretary cannot manage the meeting or packages (negative). `tests/security/owner-decisions-meetings.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; | AMB-051 | Conflict of interest | What happens when a conflict is discovered after a vote or after the authority decision?     | A tainted vote may already have produced a sanction.                                                       | Q42, Q47; DEC-F38 (purpose-scoped suspension); no rule on past votes; ADR-040 §3 items 9-12; ADR-040 §5 item 10; ADR-042 §5 (migrations 2030, 2040 and 2060, PR #67, PR #77) | R5               | Decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option B: "If a conflict is discovered after a vote/decision, mark the affected item `TAINTED_BY_CONFLICT` and block downstream progression. The committee then determines whether a re-vote is required or the result may be confirmed. Confirmation is permitted only where the conflicted participation could not have changed the substantive result, with documented reasoning." Follow-on DEC-AMB-013, decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option A: "If a conflict of interest is discovered after the competent authority has already decided, and the conflicted member's participation could have changed the substantive voting result: 1. Mark the affected vote/recommendation package `TAINTED_BY_CONFLICT`. 2. Immediately block any further downstream action based on that decision. 3. Reopen the affected committee decision round for a new vote excluding the conflicted member. 4. Recalculate quorum and eligibility for the new round. 5. Preserve the original vote, recommendation and authority decision as immutable historical records. 6. After the committee completes the valid re-vote, submit the resulting recommendation/package to the competent authority for a new decision. 7. The earlier authority decision is never edited or deleted; it is marked `SUPERSEDED`. 8. Record the reason, conflict event, affected member, original result, new result, authority re-decision and complete audit lineage. 9. No corrective action, sanction execution, decision notice or closure may proceed from the tainted decision while this remediation path is open. 10. If the conflicted vote could not have changed the result, retain the existing DEC-AMB-003 confirmation path instead; do not force a re-vote unnecessarily. Do not route this through the ordinary closed-case reopening mechanism under DEC-AMB-002. This is remediation of a procedurally tainted decision chain, not a new-evidence reopening." Follow-on DEC-AMB-014, decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option A: "The replacement authority decision must explicitly determine the disposition of every corrective action that originated from the superseded decision. For each affected action, the replacement decision must choose one of: • `RE_ADOPT` • `MODIFY` • `WAIVE` • `REPLACE_WITH_NEW_ACTION` Until that disposition is recorded, the action remains: `FROZEN_BY_SUPERSEDED_DECISION` and must not progress, be verified complete, or support case closure. Do not automatically carry the action forward. Do not automatically waive it. Do not depend on `CORRECTIVE_ACTION_WAIVE` merely to resolve this supersession scenario. This is a disposition by the replacement authority decision itself, not an ordinary standalone waiver. Audit must preserve: • original corrective action; • original authority decision; • superseding decision; • disposition selected; • reason; • actor; • timestamps; • any replacement action linkage. If re-adopted, the action resumes under the new authority decision without losing its historical execution evidence. If modified, preserve the prior version and create an auditable revised action version. If waived, retain it permanently as `WAIVED_BY_SUPERSEDING_DECISION`. Case closure remains blocked until every frozen action has a final disposition." Follow-on DEC-AMB-015, decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option A (in full under AMB-218): "Use the controlled reopening machinery of DEC-AMB-002, but treat the validated post-decision conflict event as an authorized reopening trigger alongside new evidence." Coordinator defaults the owner kept on 2026-10-13 (owner-confirmed, not new decisions): "Keep the coordinator defaults for now: 1. Sanction already executed under the superseded decision • retain the execution record; • do not automatically execute it again; • mark it as originating from a `SUPERSEDED` decision; • the replacement authority decision determines any remedial consequence separately. 2. Grievance already filed against the superseded decision • do not delete, cancel or silently migrate it; • retain it with an explicit `SUPERSEDED_DECISION` relationship/status; • preserve all submissions and audit history; • any further procedural treatment should be derived from the replacement decision/grievance rules rather than automatic mutation." | No                                                                | CRITICAL | Decided 2026-10-13 (DEC-AMB-003, DEC-AMB-013, DEC-AMB-014, DEC-AMB-015) | Decided; built in migrations 2030, 2040 and 2060 (ADR-040 §3 items 9-12; ADR-040 §5 item 10; ADR-042 §5), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14) and PR #77 (merged 2026-10-14). Migration 2030 builds DEC-AMB-003. A closed vote with a participant who is now conflicted makes the item TAINTED_BY_CONFLICT (`decision.vote_item_conflicted`, `decision.package_tainted`). Triggers and command checks then refuse adoption, legal review, submission, every authority decision except RETURN_TO_COMMITTEE, opening a corrective action and issuing a decision notice. This lasts until the secretary of an open meeting records `api.resolve_conflict_taint` (RE_VOTE or CONFIRM, with a reason of 10-2000 characters). CONFIRM is refused with `RESULT_MAY_CHANGE` unless `decision.recount_without` gives the same result. 2030 also builds DEC-AMB-013 and DEC-AMB-014. On a DECIDED package, a decisive RE_VOTE (otherwise `RESULT_UNCHANGED`) opens `decision.conflict_remediation`. A forged remediation is refused with `NO_VALIDATED_CONFLICT`; on a case that is not ACTIVE it is refused with `CASE_NOT_ACTIVE` (the DEC-AMB-015 guard). The conflicted members are kept out of the re-vote (`EXCLUDED_BY_CONFLICT`). The earlier decision is marked SUPERSEDED in the append-only `decision.authority_decision_supersession` only after the replacement exists (`REPLACEMENT_DECISION_REQUIRED`). `decision.decision_block` blocks downstream steps. Actions of a superseded decision stay `FROZEN_BY_SUPERSEDED_DECISION` until `api.dispose_superseded_corrective_action` records RE_ADOPT, MODIFY, WAIVE (`WAIVED_BY_SUPERSEDING_DECISION`) or REPLACE_WITH_NEW_ACTION. Migration 2040 holds the two kept defaults. `api.sanction_executability` marks `execution_origin: SUPERSEDED_DECISION`, and the sanction is never executed again. `api.grievance_view` marks `contested_decision_relationship: SUPERSEDED_DECISION`. Migration 2060 adds the reopening reason `DECISIVE_POST_DECISION_CONFLICT` (`api.request_case_reopening`, `api.decide_case_reopening`). It is refused with `CASE_CLOSURE_APPROVAL_RULE_REQUIRED` while that setting ships SOURCE_REQUIRED. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migration 2030. | T-AMB-028: A conflict found after a vote or decision marks the item TAINTED_BY_CONFLICT and blocks every downstream step; the committee then re-votes or confirms, and a confirmation without documented reasoning that the conflicted participation could not have changed the result is refused. Where the conflict is found after the competent authority decided (DEC-AMB-013): a decisive tainted vote cannot be confirmed; a decided package can enter this controlled re-vote path only because of the validated conflict event; the conflicted member cannot participate; the prior authority decision remains immutable and is marked superseded only after the replacement decision; downstream execution remains blocked until the new authority decision is recorded. With DEC-AMB-014, DEC-AMB-015 and the owner-confirmed defaults (the reopening test is under T-AMB-131): frozen corrective actions cannot resume without replacement-decision disposition; a closed case cannot enter DEC-AMB-013 directly; the original authority decision is immutable; superseded sanctions are not automatically re-executed; existing grievances are preserved rather than rewritten. `tests/security/recommendations-authority.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "T-AMB-028: a conflict found after a vote or decision taints the item; the database refuses every downstream step until the committee resolves it by RE_VOTE or CONFIRM; CONFIRM needs documented reasoning and a result the conflicted participation could not have changed" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).) |

Migration 2030 adds `api.reverse_meeting_conflict_determination(concern, reason)`. It writes the append-only `committee.meeting_conflict_reversal`, audited `MEETING_CONFLICT_REVERSED`, with the reason kept in the row. Only the next eligible level after the original decider in the DEC-F04 order may reverse (`committee.concern_reverser_seat`: chair, deputy chair or conflict authority, after a secretary, chair or deputy-chair decider). That is never the raiser, the subject or the original decider, and nobody when no later level is eligible. A caller who may not read the concern gets NOT_FOUND; one who may read it but is not the reverser gets FORBIDDEN; the command also refuses `NOT_UPHELD`, `ALREADY_REVERSED` and `CASE_NOT_ACTIVE`. The reversal is prospective: `committee.conflict_counts` stops counting only the conflict that rested on the reversed concern. The member may therefore declare again in later meetings (`PRIOR_CONFLICT_DECLARED` no longer applies), while the meeting of the determination keeps them CONFLICT_DECLARED. A reversal never clears a TAINTED_BY_CONFLICT taint. The meeting stores `is_emergency`, `emergency_reason` and the minimum in force, and `MEETING_SCHEDULED` records the emergency flag and the minimum, not the emergency reason. A `committee.meeting` belongs to one committee proceeding and one case (`committee_proceeding_id` and `case_id` not null, 1890), with its own participants, declarations, quorum and minutes. `authz.can_view_meeting` admits only a reader of that meeting's proceeding or a participant of that meeting who passes the case gate; since 2010, not an excluded witness. A participant of one case's meeting gets the uniform `NOT_FOUND` from `api.meeting_view` on another case's meeting, and from `api.acknowledge_meeting_confidentiality` and `api.declare_meeting_conflict` on its participants, as for an id that names none. | AMB-066 | Meetings | Who runs the meeting when the Secretary is absent?                          | No one may manage the meeting.                                   | Q24; ADR-019 §4; ADR-040 §2 item 2 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                                                                                         | R2               | The alternate secretary while recorded as acting; otherwise the meeting cannot open (fail closed).                                                                                                                                                  | No                                                            | MEDIUM   | No                               | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §2 item 2), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). Only the current SECRETARY seat holder, or an ALTERNATE_SECRETARY recorded as ACTING_SECRETARY in `committee.meeting_acting_role`, can open or run a meeting (`committee.manages_meeting` behind `authz.can_manage_meeting`). For an absent secretary the meeting's chair or acting chair records the alternate with `api.activate_acting_secretary` and a reason (basis ABSENT); without an eligible alternate it refuses `NO_ELIGIBLE_ALTERNATE` and the meeting cannot be opened. The trigger `vote_item_quorum_at_opening` (`committee.require_quorum_at_opening`) refuses `NO_QUORUM` when any vote item is opened without quorum, on every path (meeting, package and grievance votes); under a CUSTOM quorum at least one counting member must be present. `api.close_vote_item` recomputes quorum at close (`committee.quorum_state`) and records the result NO_QUORUM when it has been lost, so no APPROVED or REJECTED result is recorded then. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | T-AMB-039: Opening a vote without quorum is refused; closing after quorum loss gives NO_QUORUM (negative). `tests/security/committee-meetings.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "no vote opens without quorum (AMB-067, migration 2030)" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                       |

| AMB-074 | Quorum & voting | What is the decision rule, and what do abstentions and recusals count as? | Different counting changes outcomes.                        | DEC-F01; Q59; ADR-020; ADR-040 §1; ADR-040 §2 item 6 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                                                   | R1               | Simple majority of substantive votes: APPROVE > REJECT. ABSTAIN, ABSENT, RECUSED and UNABLE_TO_VOTE count neither way. Quorum is evaluated independently. Configurable and effective-dated per committee.               | Yes — `DECISION_RULE = SIMPLE_MAJORITY_SUBSTANTIVE (rule set)` | CRITICAL | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §1; ADR-040 §2 item 6), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). The committee rule set carries `decision_rule`, shipped `SIMPLE_MAJORITY_SUBSTANTIVE` (the only value its check constraint and `api.revise_committee_rule_set` accept), effective-dated and snapshotted per proceeding. `api.close_vote_item` checks quorum first, then APPROVE > REJECT on each member's last choice; ABSTAIN, RECUSED, ABSENT and UNABLE_TO_VOTE count neither way, and an item with no APPROVE or REJECT takes the rule's `no_substantive_vote_outcome`. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                                 | T-AMB-043: Abstentions do not change APPROVE vs REJECT outcome. `tests/security/owner-decisions-meetings.spec.ts` (EXISTS; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "abstentions count neither way: a simple majority of substantive votes decides" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Never the Secretary. | No                                                             | CRITICAL | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §2 item 2), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). On a tie `api.close_vote_item` sets AWAITING_CASTING_VOTE only when the rule's `casting_vote_enabled` is on and `committee.casting_voter` finds a caster: the chair if present, cleared and holding their own counted vote, otherwise the activated acting chair only when `acting_chair_casting_vote` is true (ships false, SOURCE_REQUIRED); otherwise the item stays TIED. `api.cast_casting_vote` refuses anyone else (`FORBIDDEN`, the secretary included) and a non-tie (`NOT_A_TIE`). | AMB-081 | Quorum & voting | May a member change their vote before the item closes?                    | A mistaken click should be correctable but not silently.    | Q60–Q63; ADR-020 (vote rows store recorded_at); ADR-040 §1; ADR-040 §2 item 6 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                          | R3               | Configurable; default allowed while OPEN, as an append-only revision; only the last counts; revisions stay secret like votes.                                                                                           | Yes — `VOTE_REVISION_BEFORE_CLOSE = true`                      | MEDIUM   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §1; ADR-040 §2 item 6), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `api.revise_vote` appends a `committee.vote_revision` row (no grant to any role) while the item is OPEN, and `committee.vote_choice` makes the last revision the choice that `api.close_vote_item` counts and `api.vote_item_status` shows after close; the ledger event `VOTE_REVISED` carries only the meeting id and revision number. The option is the rule-set column `dissent_note_allowed` (shipped default true); when off the command refuses `VOTE_NOTE_NOT_ALLOWED`. The setting is the rule-set column `dissent_note_allowed`, not a VOTE_DISSENT_NOTE_ALLOWED setting. The 10-argument `api.add_recommendation_item` takes dependencies on active items of the same package (`decision.recommendation_item_dependency`) and an item `exclusivity_group`; `api.recommendation_item_flags` flags `DEPENDENCY_NOT_APPROVED` when a dependency is withdrawn, rejected by the committee or not approved by an authority, and `EXCLUSIVITY_CONFLICT` on alternatives, and `api.record_authority_decision` refuses approving two items of one group (`EXCLUSIVITY_GROUP_VIOLATION`). The catalogue's `exclusivity_group` ships null in every seeded version and `decision.sanction_catalogue_entry` shows it as SOURCE_REQUIRED. There is no ITEM_DEPENDENCY_ENFORCEMENT setting: the behaviour is fixed. Fail-safe: no sanction is treated as effective before the DECISION notice is delivered to the subject.                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Yes — `SANCTION_EFFECTIVE_DATE_RULE = SOURCE_REQUIRED (default: delivery)`                       | CRITICAL | No                               | Partly built in migration 2040 (ADR-040 §5 item 10), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `decision.sanction_execution` rows are guarded on every path by the trigger `sanction_execution_guard` (function `decision.guard_sanction_execution`): no execution before the subject's DECISION notice is delivered (`DECISION_NOT_DELIVERED`) and no `effective_from` before the delivery (`EFFECTIVE_BEFORE_DELIVERY`). `decision.execution_block` is the one source for that guard and for `api.sanction_executability`, which reports `DECISION_NOT_DELIVERED` and never shows the sanction executable before delivery. Not built: No `SANCTION_EFFECTIVE_DATE_RULE` setting ships, and the work-regulations rule is not recorded as SOURCE_REQUIRED in the database; only the delivery fail-safe is built, fixed in the guard. Tracked as CDF-240, blocked until the work regulations arrive. A null SANCTION_EFFECTIVE_DATE_RULE setting, with no behaviour, may ride migration 2170; the rule itself takes a later migration once the source arrives. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application). | T-AMB-062: Execution state cannot show a sanction as effective before delivery (negative). `tests/security/notices-grievances-2040.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "T-AMB-062: execution never shows a sanction effective before the decision notice is delivered" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| AMB-110 | Recommendations & sanctions | Does a grievance suspend execution of the sanction?                                 | Executing an irreversible sanction before the grievance is decided may defeat the grievance. | Q94, Q95; no rule on suspension; ADR-040 §5 item 7; ADR-040 §5 item 10 (migration 2040, PR #67)      | R5               | Decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option C: "Grievance effect on sanction execution is configurable by sanction type. Irreversible/materially difficult-to-reverse sanctions remain suspended during the applicable grievance window and, if filed, until the grievance is resolved. Reversible sanctions may proceed according to the configured catalogue rule." | Yes — `SANCTION_GRIEVANCE_SUSPENSION (per sanction type) = SOURCE_REQUIRED (default: suspended)` | CRITICAL | Decided 2026-10-13 (DEC-AMB-004) | Decided; built in migration 2040 (ADR-040 §5 item 7; ADR-040 §5 item 10), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). Migration 2040 adds per-type columns to `decision.sanction_catalogue`: `is_irreversible`, `grievance_execution_rule` (SUSPEND_UNTIL_RESOLVED or PROCEED) and `rule_source_status`. Only `api.set_sanction_rule` sets them (COMMITTEE_ADMIN, recorded in the append-only `decision.sanction_rule_change`), and an irreversible type can never be set to PROCEED (`IRREVERSIBLE_SANCTION_SUSPENDED`, also a check constraint). `decision.execution_block` holds back an irreversible type, a type with no rule, or a sanction outside the catalogue. It returns `GRIEVANCE_WINDOW_OPEN` during the window and `GRIEVANCE_OPEN` while a grievance on it is RECEIVED, AWAITING_ADMISSIBILITY_DECISION or ADMITTED. A reversible type proceeds only when it is configured PROCEED. SANCTION_GRIEVANCE_SUSPENSION ships SOURCE_REQUIRED: every `grievance_execution_rule` is null and held as suspended, and TERMINATION is marked irreversible as the fail-safe default. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migration 2040.       | T-AMB-063: An irreversible or materially difficult-to-reverse sanction is not executable during the grievance window or, once a grievance is filed, until it is resolved; a reversible sanction proceeds only under its configured catalogue rule, and a type with no rule is held as irreversible. `tests/security/grievance-defence.spec.ts` (EXISTS, negative; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "T-AMB-063: an irreversible type and a type with no rule are not executable in the grievance window; a reversible type follows its rule" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).) |

| AMB-113 | Legal review | When is legal review mandatory?                                               | Missing a required review invalidates the decision.                                | DEC-F12; Q73, Q74; ADR-022 §1; ADR-040 §3 item 2 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                           | R1               | At least: configured disciplinary recommendations, litigation or legal action, matters needing legal justification, and anything referred by the Secretary or authorised owner. Configurable, effective-dated, not exhaustive.                                           | Yes — `LEGAL_REVIEW_REQUIRED_RULE (effective-dated)`        | HIGH     | No                     | Partly built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §3 item 2), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `decision.legal_review_rule` replaces the fixed type list: effective-dated rules per committee kind by item type or by the item's `requires_legal_justification` flag, seeded REQUIRED for DISCIPLINARY_SANCTION and EXTERNAL_REFERRAL (OWNER_DEFAULT) and for LEGAL_ACTION and the justification flag (OWNER_DECISION), and a catalogue version may also require review. `api.set_legal_review_rule` (COMMITTEE_ADMIN) refuses switching an owner minimum off (`OWNER_MINIMUM_RULE`), the secretary may still elect a review (`SECRETARY_ELECTED` basis), and `api.submit_to_authority` refuses `LEGAL_REVIEW_REQUIRED` without an opinion in the current round. Not built: A deliberate referral to Legal by an authorised owner other than the committee secretary: `api.request_legal_review` is open only to the package's manager (`authz.can_manage_recommendation_package`, the committee secretary). Fady decided on 2026-10-16, option B ("Wait for DoA source"): the committee secretary stays the only referrer, and who else may refer stays SOURCE_REQUIRED until the Delegation of Authority or an approved role names them; the widening then ships in migration 2160 (ADR-053). His words are quoted at the end of AMBIGUITY_REGISTER_200.md and in OWNER_DECISIONS_FROM_AMBIGUITIES.md. ### K. Competent authority

| ID      | Domain              | Ambiguity                                                                                 | Why It Matters                                                      | Existing Evidence                                                                                                                         | Resolution Class | Proposed Resolution                                                                                                                                                                                | Configurable? | ------- | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| AMB-125 | Competent authority | Who decides when the competent authority is unavailable or has delegated?                 | A decision by someone without authority is void.                    | Q78, Q84, Q85; DEC-F13 (action type → authority rule); ADR-040 §3 item 4 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                         | R4               | The Delegation of Authority is SOURCE_REQUIRED. The secretary records the deciding person's label, the delegation reference and the source document; the authority type must still match the rule. | Yes — `AUTHORITY_DELEGATION_SOURCE = SOURCE_REQUIRED`              | HIGH   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §3 item 4), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). The 13-argument `api.record_authority_decision` (2030) records the deciding person's label, an optional delegation reference (`decision.authority_decision.delegation_reference`, 3–300 characters, flagged `DELEGATED` in `decision.authority_decision_overview`) and the source document, which stays mandatory (N15, refused `INVALID` on `source_document_reference`). The authority type must still be one the submission requires (`AUTHORITY_TYPE_MISMATCH`). There is no AUTHORITY_DELEGATION_SOURCE setting: the Delegation of Authority is marked SOURCE_REQUIRED in a column comment, and the delegation reference is optional. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                               | T-AMB-073: A decision recorded without a source document is refused (N15). `tests/security/recommendations-authority.spec.ts` (EXISTS; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "T-AMB-073: needs the source document and the authority the rules name; partial approval names every item" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| AMB-126 | Competent authority | What if one package needs decisions from different authorities?                           | A single decision record cannot carry two authority types.          | Q84, Q85; DEC-F13; ADR-023 (AUTHORITY_TYPE_MISMATCH per item); ADR-040 §3 item 4 (migration 2030, PR #67)                                 | R2               | One decision record per authority type, each covering its items; the package becomes DECIDED only when every adopted item has a decision.                                                          | No                                                                 | HIGH   | No                     | Built in migration 2030 (ADR-040 §3 item 4), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). Submission writes one `decision.authority_requirement` per adopted item and required authority; each `api.record_authority_decision` covers only its own authority's undecided items (`decision.authority_decision_scope`), refusing another authority (`AUTHORITY_TYPE_MISMATCH`) or a second decision (`AUTHORITY_ALREADY_DECIDED`). The package becomes DECIDED only once every requirement of the round is decided without disagreement, and stays AWAITING_AUTHORITY until then unless an authority returns it (a RETURN_TO_COMMITTEE outcome, or a completed round whose authorities disagree on an item, sets RETURNED_BY_AUTHORITY). Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                             | T-AMB-074: A package is not DECIDED while an item lacks its authority's decision. `tests/security/recommendations-authority.spec.ts` (EXISTS; note: Re-checked on main 0f59062: "T-AMB-074: a package is not DECIDED while an item lacks its own authority's decision (AMB-126)" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

The `text[]` overload of `api.set_action_authority_rule` (COMMITTEE_ADMIN) names one to four authority types; the first is the rule's own and the rest go to `decision.action_authority_rule_joint`, while the single-authority form stays the default. Submission requires a decision from every named authority, and when joint authorities' item outcomes differ the package goes to RETURNED_BY_AUTHORITY instead of DECIDED. There is no AUTHORITY_RULE.required_types setting: the joint list is the rule's own data. Migration 2040 adds two commands. `api.file_grievance_reconsideration` is for the grievant, through the portal. `api.record_grievance_reconsideration` is for the handling Grievance Committee secretary, and its submission date may not fall before the decided grievance's outcome. Both are allowed only once the grievance is DECIDED (`GRIEVANCE_NOT_DECIDED`) and only with a new-evidence description and reference (`grievance.grievance.reconsideration_of`, `new_evidence_description`, `new_evidence_reference`). The trigger `grievance.guard_grievance_scope` refuses, on every path, a second reconsideration or any further grievance on the same decision (`RECONSIDERATION_USED`), and a plain new grievance on an already decided decision (`GRIEVANCE_ALREADY_DECIDED`). Admissibility must confirm that the evidence is new (`NEW_EVIDENCE_NOT_CONFIRMED`). | AMB-146 | Grievance | When is a decision final?                                                                                                                 | Closure and records need a finality point.                                                                                                         | ADR-037 (window end stored; GRIEVANCE_FINALITY flag PLANNED); DEC-F40; ADR-042 §3 (migrations 2060 and 2070, PR #77, PR #80)                                                                                  | R2               | Final when the window ended with no grievance, or the grievance outcome is recorded and DEC-AMB-001 allows nothing further.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | No                                                                                                                     | HIGH     | No                               | Partly built in migrations 2060 and 2070 (ADR-042 §3), on main since PR #77 (merged 2026-10-14) and PR #80 (merged 2026-10-15). Migration 2060 adds `case_mgmt.closure_blocks` and a BEFORE trigger on every ACTIVE→CLOSED change (`case_mgmt.guard_case_readiness`), so it applies on every path, including `api.close_case` and the CDF_CASE_V1 steps. Closure is refused with `GRIEVANCE_WINDOW_OPEN` while a DECISION notice's grievance window has not started or has not ended. It is refused with `DECISION_NOT_FINAL` while a grievance or reconsideration is RECEIVED, AWAITING_ADMISSIBILITY_DECISION or ADMITTED, or while a package sits between the committee's vote and the authority's decision (VOTING, ADOPTED, IN_LEGAL_REVIEW or AWAITING_AUTHORITY). Not built: No finality point is stored or derived: the GRIEVANCE_FINALITY flag (FINAL_NOT_GRIEVABLE) stays PLANNED in the 1970 catalogue. DEC-AMB-001 has no reconsideration period (it is SOURCE_REQUIRED and none was invented), so a decided grievance whose one reconsideration is still unused does not hold closure. In effect, finality is read as 'nothing is open', not as 'DEC-AMB-001 allows nothing further'. Tracked as CDF-246. Fady decided on 2026-10-16, option A ("Nothing open"): CASE_CLOSURE_FINALITY = no open or pending reconsideration, grievance, proceeding, or other configured closure blocker; no default period is invented. Closure already holds while one is open (2060), so only the derived finality read model remains, placed in migration 2170. His words are quoted at the end of AMBIGUITY_REGISTER_200.md and in OWNER_DECISIONS_FROM_AMBIGUITIES.md. Under it the Secretary's INADMISSIBLE in `api.assess_grievance_admissibility` is only an assessment (AWAITING_ADMISSIBILITY_DECISION) and the Secretary's `api.decide_grievance_admissibility` is refused (`FORBIDDEN`); the committee's decision is entered by the Grievance Committee chair or deputy chair with a decision reference (`INVALID` on `decision_reference` without one), while an admission stays with the Secretary. | AMB-149 | Grievance | Can a grievance outcome make the sanction more severe?                                                                                    | Fear of a harsher outcome deters grievances.                                                                                                       | Q95 (modify); no rule; ADR-040 §5 item 9 (migration 2040, PR #67)                                                                                                                                             | R5               | Decided by the owner on 2026-10-13, option A: "A grievance outcome must never make the challenged disciplinary sanction more severe. It may uphold, reduce, modify in a non-more-severe manner, or cancel it. If genuinely new facts justify a potentially more severe sanction, that must proceed through the appropriate reinvestigation/new decision process with full due-process protections rather than through the grievance itself."                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Yes — `SANCTION_SEVERITY_RANK (per catalogue entry) = SOURCE_REQUIRED (default: unranked, so no change between types)` | HIGH     | Decided 2026-10-13 (DEC-AMB-011) | Decided; built in migration 2040 (ADR-040 §5 item 9), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). Migration 2040 adds `decision.sanction_catalogue.severity_rank` (SANCTION_SEVERITY_RANK, set by `api.set_sanction_rule`) and the append-only `grievance.outcome_substitution`. `api.record_grievance_outcome` writes it only for a MODIFY outcome with substitutions, and the trigger `grievance.guard_outcome_substitution` guards it on every path. A change between types needs both ranks and a substitute no more severe (`SANCTION_MORE_SEVERE`; an unranked type gives `SANCTION_NOT_COMPARABLE`). Within one type the outcome declares REDUCE or NONE, and INCREASE is refused with `SANCTION_MORE_SEVERE`. Uphold, cancel and RETURN_FOR_REINVESTIGATION remain available; after a cancel, execution is refused with `SANCTION_SET_ASIDE_BY_GRIEVANCE`. SANCTION_SEVERITY_RANK ships SOURCE_REQUIRED with every type unranked, so no change between types is accepted until ranks are configured. | AMB-251 | Grievance | Does the Restricted Case Authority name a grievance admissibility decider on a restricted case where no seated decider can view the case? | Where no seated decider can view a restricted case, nobody can decide admissibility, and asking someone who cannot see the case would disclose it. | AMB-148 (DEC-F17, admissibility decider); ADR-040 item 17 and its "Still SOURCE_REQUIRED" list, migration 2040 (rebase branch); ADR-040 §5 item 17; ADR-040, "Still SOURCE_REQUIRED" (migration 2040, PR #67) | R4               | SOURCE_REQUIRED: whether the Restricted Case Authority names a decider, to come from the Grievance Committee charter (inferred here; confirm when the source arrives). Fail closed: where no seated decider can view the restricted case, nobody is asked and the handling secretary keeps the grievance. The admissibility decider is whoever holds a CHAIR, DEPUTY_CHAIR or MEMBER seat on the Grievance Committee in the registry when the decision is due, as the grievance's decider rule names (CHAIR rule: chair or deputy chair; COMMITTEE rule: chair, deputy chair or a member). No proceeding exists yet at this stage, so no snapshot applies. Alternates never decide admissibility or read as deciders. The decider must also pass the case gate, have taken no part in the Investigation Committee and, on a restricted case, already be able to view the case. | Yes — `RESTRICTED_GRIEVANCE_DECIDER_NOMINATION = SOURCE_REQUIRED`                                                      | HIGH     | No                               | Partly built in migration 2040 (ADR-040 §5 item 17; ADR-040, "Still SOURCE_REQUIRED"), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `grievance.is_pending_decider` (and `authz.is_grievance_decider` for the caller) admits only a current Grievance Committee CHAIR or DEPUTY_CHAIR (CHAIR rule) or CHAIR, DEPUTY_CHAIR or MEMBER (COMMITTEE rule) seat holder from the registry, never an alternate, who passes `authz.user_case_gate`, took no part in the Investigation Committee and, on a restricted case, can already view it (`authz.user_can_view_case`). The decider read policies and `notify.on_grievance_awaiting_decision` (`GRIEVANCE_ADMISSIBILITY_DECISION_REQUESTED`) use it, so where no seated decider can view a restricted case nobody is asked or reads it and the grievance stays AWAITING_ADMISSIBILITY_DECISION with its handling secretary; no nomination by the Restricted Case Authority exists. Not built: `api.decide_grievance_admissibility` itself admits a CHAIR or DEPUTY_CHAIR who passes the seat, `authz.user_case_gate` (clearance and no conflict) and G3 checks without checking that they can view a restricted case, so the restricted-case condition binds the notice and the reads but not the command. The sourced nomination rule (`RESTRICTED_GRIEVANCE_DECIDER_NOMINATION`) stays SOURCE_REQUIRED, with no setting, by design. Tracked as CDF-237 (Security LOW), placed by the coordinator as migration 2098. Owner: Rebase PR-A, migrations 2030/2040 (DEC-F01–F41 application).                                                                                                                                                                                        | T-AMB-151: Where no seated decider can view a restricted case, nobody is asked to decide admissibility and no seat holder who cannot already see the case is told or reads it (fail closed). `tests/security/purpose-read-models-2040.spec.ts` (EXTEND, negative; missing: A seat holder cleared for the case's classification but not working the restricted case: the test's chair and member are CONFIDENTIAL-cleared on SECRET case 0003, so the case gate (clearance) already excludes them and the restricted-case condition is not isolated, for the reads, the notice or the command. Found on main 0f59062: "T-AMB-106: on a restricted case no seat holder who cannot already see the case is told or reads it (fail closed)" (PR #67, merged 2026-10-14).) |

The default period is effective-dated configuration in `grievance.defence_response_period`, seeded with one version whose `default_days` is null and `source_status` SOURCE_REQUIRED (no period invented). `api.issue_defence_notice` uses the period in force when no due date is entered; with neither it refuses `CONFLICT:RESPONSE_PERIOD_SOURCE_REQUIRED`, and an entered date in the past is refused `INVALID:due_at`. The audit event `DEFENCE_NOTICE_ISSUED` records `default_period_days`. The period is a table, not a DEFENCE_RESPONSE_DAYS setting. | AMB-152 | Employee defence & hearings | How are extensions, suspensions and rescheduling of the defence period handled? | A due date fixed at issue cannot be extended today.                        | DEC-F18; 1910 defence_notice.due_at (immutable); ADR-040 §5 item 4 (migration 2040, PR #67)                        | R1               | Supported with reason and audit; each change is an append-only event; the original date is kept.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Yes — `DEFENCE_EXTENSION_MAX_DAYS = SOURCE_REQUIRED`   | HIGH     | No                               | Partly built in migration 2040 (ADR-040 §5 item 4), on main since PR #67 (merged 2026-10-14). `api.change_defence_deadline` records an EXTENSION, SUSPENSION, RESUMPTION or RESCHEDULE of an open defence notice with a reason (10–2000 characters) as an append-only row in `grievance.defence_deadline_change`, whose first `previous_due_at` keeps the original date, and audits `DEFENCE_DEADLINE_CHANGED` with ids, kind and dates. It refuses `NOT_A_DEFENCE_NOTICE`, `NOTICE_NOT_OPEN`, `NOT_SUSPENDED`, `DEADLINE_SUSPENDED` and an extension that does not move the date later (`INVALID:new_due_at`); `grievance.protect_records` lets `due_at` change only to match the latest recorded change, and reminders and lateness skip a suspended notice. Not built: No `DEFENCE_EXTENSION_MAX_DAYS` setting exists: an extension or reschedule has no configurable maximum.
